STATE OF NEW YORK 
BEFORE THE 
PUBLIC SERVICE COMMISSION
Case 21-G-0394 – Proceeding on Motion of the Commission as to the Rates, Charges, Rules and 
Regulations of Corning Natural Gas Corporation for Gas Service 
Case 21-G-0260 – Joint Petition of Corning Natural Gas Holding Corporation, Corning Natural 
Gas Corporation, ACP Crotona Corp. and ACP Crotona Merger Sub Corp. 
for Approval, Pursuant to Section 70 of the New York Public Service Law, of 
the Merger of ACP Crotona Merger Sub Corp. into Corning Natural Gas 
Holding Corporation with Corning Natural Gas Holding Corporation as the 
Surviving Corporation and Wholly-Owned Subsidiary of ACP Crotona Corp. 
JOINT PROPOSAL 
I.
INTRODUCTION
This Joint Proposal is made as of the 14th day of March, 2022, by and among Corning
Natural Gas Corporation (“Corning LDC” or the “Company”), Corning Natural Gas Holding 
Corporation (“Corning Holdco”), ACP Crotona Corp. (“ACP Crotona”), ACP Crotona Merger 
Sub Corp. (“ACP Merger Sub”) and the Staff of the Department of Public Service (“Staff”) 
(collectively, the “Signatory Parties”).  Four other parties, the Utility Intervention Unit, Division 
of Consumer Protection, of the Department of State (“UIU”), the Public Utility Law Project 
(“PULP”), Multiple Intervenors (“MI”), and the Village of Bath Electric, Gas and Water Systems 
(“BEGWS”) (collectively, with the Signatory Parties, the “Parties”), also participated in the 
settlement negotiations.  One of these Parties, MI, supports Article V of this Joint Proposal as it 
pertains to Case 21-G-0260 and neither supports nor opposes the other provisions of the Joint 
Proposal as they pertain to Case 21-G-0394.  Two of the remaining three Parties, UIU and 
BEGWS, are not subscribing to the Joint Proposal with respect to either case, but have stated that 


--- Page 8 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
2 
they do not oppose the Joint Proposal.  The third, PULP, has not stated its final position as of the 
time of this filing.  To the extent indicated, therefore, this Joint Proposal settles all contested 
issues in the above-captioned cases among the Signatory Parties.1
II.
PROCEDURAL HISTORY 
A.
Merger Proceeding 
On May 3, 2021, Corning Holdco, Corning LDC, ACP Crotona and ACP Merger Sub 
(collectively, the “Joint Petitioners”) filed with the New York State Public Service Commission 
(the “Commission”) a verified Joint Petition (the “Petition” or the “Merger Filing”), dated April 
30, 2021, seeking Commission approval, pursuant to Section 70 of the Public Service Law 
(“PSL”), of the acquisition of Corning Holdco by ACP Crotona (the “Merger Transaction”).  
More specifically, the Petition sought approval of the acquisition of all of the outstanding 
common stock of Corning Holdco by ACP Crotona, resulting in ownership of Corning Holdco, 
Corning Holdco’s New York regulated subsidiary, Corning LDC, Corning Holdco’s partially 
owned, dormant New York affiliate, Leatherstocking Gas Company of New York, Inc.,2 three of 
Corning Holdco’s non-New York subsidiaries, Pike County Light & Power Company, 
Leatherstocking Gas Company, LLC, and Leatherstocking Pipeline Company, LLC, and Corning 
Holdco’s dormant subsidiary, Corning Natural Gas Appliance Corporation, by ACP Crotona. 
The Merger Filing was supported by prepared testimony and exhibits constituting the 
Joint Petitioners’ direct case.  Upon receipt of the Merger Filing, the Commission instituted Case 
1  
Case 21-G-0394 is referred to herein as the “Rate Proceeding” or “Rate Case”; Case 21-G-0260 is referred to 
herein as the “Merger Proceeding” or “Merger Case.”  For those portions of this Joint Proposal to which MI 
subscribes, that Party will be deemed a Signatory Party. 
2  
Since the filing of the Petition, Corning Holdco’s remaining interest in Leatherstocking Gas Company of New 
York, Inc. has been sold. 


--- Page 9 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
3 
21-G-0260 to consider the Joint Petitioners’ filing.  Between June and December of 2021, Staff 
propounded, and the Joint Petitioners responded to, 75 separately numbered interrogatories.  On 
December 21, 2021, Staff filed its Position Paper Regarding Proposed Merger (the “Position 
Paper”), in which Staff recommended that the Commission only authorize the Merger 
Transaction if certain conditions described therein were met.3  The Joint Petitioners’ response to 
the Position Paper was presented in the context of the settlement negotiations described in 
Section II.C, below. 
B.
Corning LDC Rate Proceeding 
On July 16, 2021, Corning LDC filed with the Commission revised tariff leaves as part of 
a multi-year rate filing (the “Rate Filing”) intended to increase revenues from gas operations by a 
levelized amount of $3,591,855 in each of three annual periods, commencing July 1, 2022 and 
continuing through June 30, 2025.  Absent the levelized approach, the required increases for 
these three years would have been $5,758,453, $1,643,355 and $989,061, respectively.  The 
Company indicated that it was open to developing a Safety and Reliability Charge (“SRC”) for 
the 12 months ending June 30, 2026 and June 30, 2027 which would permit recovery of carrying 
costs (defined as pre-tax overall rate of return, depreciation expense and property taxes) on 
incremental plant additions over the previous year.  The “Test Year” supporting Corning’s Rate 
Filing is the 12-month period ended March 31, 2021. 
The Rate Filing was supported by prepared testimony and exhibits constituting Corning’s 
direct case.  Following receipt of the Rate Filing, the Commission, by Notice issued on July 23, 
3  
Portions of the Position Paper contain information deemed confidential pursuant to the Commission’s 
regulations (16 NYCRR § 6-1.3).  Confidential treatment was requested for the unredacted version of the 
Position Paper. 


--- Page 10 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
4 
2021, suspended the effective date of the Rate Filing through December 12, 2021 and instituted 
Case 21-G-0394 to consider the Company’s filing.  Administrative Law Judge (“ALJ”) Gregg 
Sayre was appointed to preside over the Rate Proceeding.4  Procedural and Technical 
Conferences were held remotely on August 31, 2021, after which, on September 7, 2021, ALJ 
Sayre issued a Ruling Establishing Case Schedule geared to the commencement of evidentiary 
hearings on January 5, 2022.  Pursuant to that schedule, Corning LDC filed updates and 
corrections to its direct case on September 24, 2021, which were amended on September 30, 
2021 and October 8, 2021.  By Notice issued on September 30, 2021, the Commission further 
suspended the effective date of the Rate Filing through June 12, 2022.  Two virtual public 
statement hearings were held on the afternoon and evening of October 27, 2021.  On November 
12, 2021, Staff and MI filed testimony and exhibits in response to the Company’s Rate Filing.  
Staff recommended a one-year base delivery rate increase of $2,903,826.  On December 10, 
2021, Corning LDC and MI filed rebuttal testimony and exhibits and, on December 13, 2021, the 
Company issued a correction to its filing.  The Company’s rebuttal filing, as corrected, reflected 
a non-levelized one-year revenue increase of $5,814,058. 
Between June of 2021 and January of 2022, Staff propounded, and Corning LDC 
responded to, 381 separately numbered interrogatories.5  In October of 2021, MI propounded, 
and Corning LDC responded to, five interrogatories.  In December of 2021, UIU propounded, 
and the Company responded to, three interrogatories.  During November of 2021, the Company 
propounded, and Staff responded to, nine interrogatories. 
4  
Judge Sayre was later appointed to preside over the Merger Proceeding as well.

described in the Direct Testimony of Charles Lenns at page CL-4.

C.
Settlement Negotiations 
On November 8, 2021, the Joint Petitioners filed with the Commission a Notice of 
Impending Settlement Negotiations in the Merger Proceeding pursuant to the Commission’s 
regulations governing settlement negotiations.6  A similar Notice was filed by Corning LDC on 
the same day in the Rate Proceeding.  Pursuant to the two notices, an initial settlement 
conference was held remotely for both proceedings on December 20, 2021.  During that 
settlement conference, the parties concluded that the prospects for reaching a negotiated 
settlement were sufficiently promising to warrant contacting the ALJ to propose a postponement 
of the evidentiary hearings scheduled to commence in the Rate Proceeding on January 5, 2022.  
On December 21, 2021, an informal procedural conference call was held among Judge Sayre and 
most of the parties to the negotiations at which a three-week postponement of the evidentiary 
hearings, to January 26, 2022, was agreed upon, along with a status conference to be held on 
January 21, 2022, to determine whether settlement negotiations would continue or whether a 
litigation track would be pursued.  By Ruling Modifying Hearing Schedule and Notice of 
Procedural Conference issued December 23, 2021, the foregoing procedural modifications were 
adopted. 
Between the issuance of the aforementioned Ruling and January 21, 2022, the Parties 
exchanged settlement proposals and held three additional settlement conferences, including a 
technical conference, on December 30, 2021, and January 12 and 19, 2022.  The Parties 
continued to make progress and, prior to the scheduled January 21, 2022 Procedural Conference, 
proposed that the schedule again be modified to accommodate continued negotiations.  Based on 
6  
16 NYCRR § 3.9(a). 


--- Page 12 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal

the Parties’ recommendation and the discussion at the Procedural Conference, Judge Sayre 
issued a Ruling Modifying Procedural Schedule on January 28, 2022.  The modified schedule 
provided that, if a Joint Proposal resolving the Rate Case were filed by March 15, 2022, (a) 
statements on the Joint Proposal would be due on April 12, 2022 and responsive statements, if 
requested of, and allowed by, the ALJ, would be due no later than May 2, 2022; (b) an 
evidentiary hearing would commence on May 3, 2022; (c) if the Joint Proposal were filed 
substantially before March 15, 2022, the preceding dates would be adjusted earlier, 
corresponding with the earlier filing; and (d) if the Joint Proposal is scheduled for consideration 
at the Commission’s June 2022 Session, Corning will, if requested, agree to extend the effective 
date of its tariffs to June 30, 2022, with customary make-whole provisions for delayed 
implementation of rates beyond June 12, 2022.  The Ruling also provided that, if the Joint 
Proposal were not filed by March 15, 2022, (a) evidentiary hearings would commence on March 
22, 2022; (b) initial briefs to the ALJ would be due on April 22, 2022; (c) reply briefs to the ALJ 
would be due on May 13, 2022; and (d) Corning LDC will, if requested, agree to extend the 
effective date of its tariffs to a date specified by the ALJ or the Secretary to the Commission, no 
later than October 1, 2022, with customary make-whole provisions for delayed implementation 
of rates beyond June 12, 2022.7
After the January 21, 2022 Procedural Conference, the Parties continued to exchange 
settlement proposals and met remotely on eight separate days – January 26, 28 and 31, 2022, and 
February 4, 7, 14, 16 and 18.  An agreement in principle to resolve all issues in both proceedings 
7  
Since this Joint Proposal is being filed on March 14, 2022, the extension to October 1, 2022 is not applicable 
and the operative extension, if requested by the ALJ or the Secretary, is the aforementioned extension to June 
30, 2022.

was achieved on February 18, 2022.  Drafts of this Joint Proposal and its Appendices were

the Commission for its consideration. 
III.
APPROVAL OF THE JOINT PROPOSAL IS IN THE PUBLIC INTEREST
The Signatory Parties recommend that the Commission approve the terms of this Joint 
Proposal without modification.  The Signatory Parties have concluded that the terms and 
conditions herein resolve all issues raised in the Rate Case and the Merger Case in a manner that:  
(1) allows Corning LDC to provide safe and adequate service at just and reasonable rates 
pursuant to PSL § 65; and (2) provides for the acquisition of Corning Holdco by ACP Crotona in 
a manner that is in the “public interest” pursuant to PSL § 70.  The bases for these conclusions 
are presented in detail in the sections of this Joint Proposal that follow. 
IV.
TERMS GOVERNING THE RATE CASE 
A.
Term and Effective Date of Rate Changes 
The term of this Joint Proposal, as it pertains to the Rate Proceeding, is three years, 
commencing July 1, 2022 and continuing through June 30, 2025 (the “Rate Period” or “Rate 
Plan”).  For purposes of this Joint Proposal, Rate Year 1 means July 1, 2022 through June 30, 
2023, Rate Year 2 means July 1, 2023 through June 30, 2024 and Rate Year 3 means July 1, 
2024 through June 30, 2025.  Except as otherwise specified herein, all provisions of this Joint 
Proposal, as they pertain to rates, remain in effect until superseding rates become effective, or 
until such provisions are modified by the Commission. 
B.
Rate Plan 
1.
Summary of the Overall Revenue Requirement 
The Signatory Parties agree upon expense and revenue adjustments to the Rate Filing to 


--- Page 14 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
8 
be allowed as the basis for calculating revenue requirement, the result of which is set forth in 
Appendix A attached hereto and made a part of this Joint Proposal.  In addition to reflecting 
approximately $300,000 per year in cost savings expected to result from the Merger Transaction, 
the revenue requirement reflects the agreement by the Joint Petitioners to provide a Positive 
Benefit Adjustment (“PBA”) of $1,200,000 over the three years of the Rate Plan.8  The Signatory 
Parties agree upon a calculation of revenue requirement to be allowed as the basis for setting 
rates, which is set forth in Appendix A.  The calculation of revenue requirement yields increases 
in gas delivery service revenues of:  
Rate Year 1:  $2,533,786            
Rate Year 2:  $1,057,740 
Rate Year 3:  $741,884 
While the Signatory Parties worked to minimize the rate increases needed for safe and 
adequate service, they recognize that a significant increase in Rate Year 1, as compared to Rate 
Years 2 and 3, might place too much of a burden on ratepayers during the first year.  To address 
this concern, the Signatory Parties propose that the revenue requirement increase be levelized 
over the three Rate Years as follows: 
Rate Year 1:  $1,706,870 
Rate Year 2:  $1,798,620 
Rate Year 3:  $1,740,870 
The calculation of the levelized amounts for each Rate Year, including the impact of carrying 
8  
As described in Section V.A.8.b, below, of the $400,000 annual amount of the PBA, $330,000 is to be used to 
offset the otherwise applicable revenue requirement in each year and $70,000 is to be used to fund an increase 
in Low Income Program costs in each year. 


--- Page 15 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
9 
charges, is shown in Appendix B.  Base rates will be changed by the stand-alone revenue 
requirements indicated above and the surcharge credit/debit mechanism will be used to achieve 
the levelized amounts for Rate Years 1, 2 and 3.  The calculation of the surcharge credit/debit by 
customer class is detailed in Appendix C.  The surcharge credit/debit mechanism to effect the 
levelization amount will cease at the end of Rate Year 3 (i.e., on July 1, 2025). 
To the extent Commission approval of Rate Year 1 rates occurs after a date that would 
permit such rates to go into effect by June 12, 2022, the Company has requested, and the 
Signatory Parties have agreed to, a make-whole provision whereby the Company will recover 
shortfalls and refund over-collections such that the Company and its customers would be in the 
same position had Rate Year 1 rates gone into effect on June 12, 2022.9  Revenue adjustments 
resulting from the make-whole provision will be deferred for future recovery, with interest 
calculated at the capitalization-weighted cost of debt which, as prescribed in Section IV.B.5.b, 
below, shall be 2.09%. 
2.
Estimated Sales and Revenues 
a.
Sales Forecast 
The overall sales forecasts associated with the revenue forecasts that underlie this Joint 
Proposal are set forth in Appendix D.  A ten-year weather normalization was used in the 
preparation of those forecasts.
9 
Revenue adjustments will be calculated as the difference between:  (1) sales revenues the Company would 
have billed at the new rates beginning June 12, 2022 and the date new rates actually go into effect; and (2) the 
same level of sales revenues at current rates.  The revenue adjustments would include all applicable surcharges 
and carrying charges and would be subject to reconciliation in accordance with all applicable mechanisms, 
such as the Revenue Decoupling Mechanism (“RDM”). 


--- Page 16 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
10 
b.
Local Production and Contract Revenues 
i.
Local Production Access and Meter Charges 
The local production revenue imputation for access and meter charges shall be $152,693 
in each Rate Year and any deviation from such imputed amount (above or below) shall be shared 
80%/20% between customers and shareholders, respectively, subject to the sharing mechanism 
being reset as described below in Section IV.B.2.b.iii. 
ii.
Local Production Transportation Charges 
The local production transportation revenues shall continue to be shared 80%/20% 
between customers and shareholders, subject to the sharing mechanism being reset as described 
below in Section IV.B.2.b.iii.  Because no transportation revenues are anticipated during the Rate 
Period, there will be no imputation of such revenues in base rates. 
iii.
Local Production Sharing Mechanism Reset 
Once the annual shareholders’ 20% share from access and meter charges, together with 
transportation charges, exceeds $200,000, the sharing mechanism between customers and 
shareholders shall be reset to 90% to customers and 10% to shareholders for all revenues above 
$200,000 annually. 
iv.
Contract Revenues 
The revenue imputation for Contracts 1 through 7, NYSE&G, Empire and Stand shall be 
$1,898,472 for Rate Year 1, $1,965,597 for Rate Year 2 and $2,031,266 for Rate Year 3, as 
detailed in Appendix R, and any deviation from such imputed amounts (above or below) shall be 
shared 90%/10% between customers and shareholders, respectively.

c.
Revenues for Each Rate Year at Current Rates 
The Signatory Parties agree to the delivery revenues shown in Appendix E for Rate

Appendix F details the components of the Merchant Function Charge (“MFC”) targets to be used 
until changed by the Commission.
d.

Cost of Capital 
a.
Capital Structure and Return on Equity 
The cost of capital for Corning LDC during the Rate Period under this Joint Proposal 
shall be based on a capital structure of 48.00% common equity, as shown in Appendix L.  
Corning LDC’s allowed Return on Equity (“ROE”) shall be 9.25%. 
b.
Cost of Long-Term Debt 
The cost of capital for Corning LDC during the Rate Period shall be based on a weighted 
cost of long-term debt equal to 4.04% during Rate Year 1, 4.10% during Rate Year 2, and 4.15% 
during Rate Year 3, with no true-up to actual interest costs.    The cost recovery for Corning 
LDC’s variable rate loan, issued on October 31, 2021, shall be permanently capped at 4.41% in 
all future rate proceedings, so long as it remains an outstanding obligation of the Company.  This 
shall be the case even if future interest rates should rise above that amount.


--- Page 24 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
18 
c.
Earnings Sharing Mechanism 
Corning LDC shall be subject to an Earnings Sharing Mechanism (“ESM”), described 
below.  For purposes of the ESM, Corning LDC shall file, no later than 90 days following the 
end of each Rate Year, a computation, together with supporting work papers in the same form as 
shown in Appendix M of the Company’s earned ROE for that Rate Year.  The ESM will be 
calculated on an annual basis.  In the event that Corning LDC’s earned ROE in any of the three 
Rate Years included in the Rate Period exceeds an earnings threshold of 9.75%, the amount in 
excess of 9.75% shall be deemed shared earnings. 
The parameters of the ESM shall reflect the following sharing thresholds and bands:  
(a) the Company will be permitted to retain all earnings up to and including a ROE of 9.75%, 
(b) 50% of the revenue requirement equivalent of any shared earnings in excess of 9.75% and up 
to and including 10.25% shall be deferred for the benefit of customers, and the remaining 50% of 
any such earnings will be retained by Corning LDC, (c) 75% of the revenue requirement 
equivalent of any shared earnings in excess of 10.25% and up to and including 10.75% shall be 
deferred for the benefit of customers, and the remaining 25% of any such earnings will be 
retained by Corning LDC, and (d) 90% of the revenue requirement equivalent of any shared 
earnings in excess of 10.75% shall be deferred for the benefit of customers, and the remaining 
10% of any such earnings will be retained by Corning LDC.   
The calculation of the ROE for ESM purposes is demonstrated in Appendix M, 
Schedule 1.  The earned ROE shall be calculated from Corning LDC’s books of account for the 
applicable Rate Year.  Corning LDC shall calculate its ROE by excluding the effects of the 
following: 
1) Amounts previously booked in excess of earnings thresholds. 


--- Page 25 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
19 
2) Surcharges and surcredits related to levelization. 
3) Positive and Negative Revenue Adjustments pursuant to the gas safety and 
customer service mechanisms. 
4) Positive and Negative Revenue Adjustments pursuant to performance incentives 
and leak prone pipe incentives. 
5) Interest on Temporary Cash Investments. 
6) All other amounts charged to Other Income and Deductions consistent with the 
Uniform System of Accounts not considered part of the cost of service. 
7) Costs relating to non-qualified pension plans. 
8) Other Comprehensive Income from Common Equity. 
9) Company’s share of tax refunds. 
10) Lost and Unaccounted for Gas. 
11) Any other Commission-approved ratemaking incentives and revenue adjustments 
in effect during the applicable Rate Year. 
The specific exclusions are also illustrated in Appendix M, Schedule 2. 
The earnings computations shall reflect the lesser of (a) an equity ratio of 50.00% or 
(b) the Company’s actual average common equity ratio.  The actual average common equity ratio 
shall exclude all components related to “other comprehensive income” that may be required by 
generally accepted accounting principles; such charges are recognized for financial accounting 
reporting purposes, but are not recognized or realized for ratemaking purposes.     
If the Company does not file for new base delivery rates to take effect within 30 days 
after the expiration of Rate Year 3, the ESM shall continue to be calculated on an annual basis 
until new rates are approved by the Commission and become effective.  In the event of a partial 
year or “stub” period, actual average rate base for that period will be adjusted by an operating 
income ratio factor.  This adjustment to rate base is intended to align operating income to the 
level of rate base that generated that income.  The operating income ratio factor will be 
calculated as the ratio of operating income during the same partial period in the previous Rate 
Year to the total operating income for that Rate Year.  An example of the calculation for the stub 
period is shown in Appendix M, Schedule 3. 


--- Page 26 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
20 
Amounts allocated to customers under the ESM shall be deferred on Corning LDC’s 
books for future disposition by the Commission.  The shared earnings deferred for the benefit of 
customers shall accrue carrying charges at the Company’s authorized pre-tax rate of return. 
6.
Gas Safety - Regulatory Goals 
a.
Leak Backlog 
For calendar years (“CY”) 2022, 2023 and 2024, Corning LDC shall be assessed a 
Regulatory Liability of ten Basis Points (“BP”) of ROE12 if the Company has two or more 
Type 1, 2 or 2A leaks in backlog pending repair, including repairs that failed re-checks, on 
December 31 of the respective year.  The Company will be assessed a Regulatory Liability of 
five BP if the total number of Type 1, 2, 2A and 3 leaks in backlog pending repair, including 
repairs that failed re-checks, on December 31 of the respective year is 35 leaks or greater.  If 
Corning LDC maintains fewer than 34 total leaks, but not fewer than 26 total leaks at calendar 
year-end for any of the three CY (2022, 2023 or 2024), the Company will earn a Positive 
Revenue Adjustment (“PRA”) of two BP; if the Company maintains 25 or fewer total leaks, but 
not fewer than 16 total leaks at calendar year-end, it will earn a PRA of four BPs; if the 
Company maintains 15 or fewer total leaks at calendar year-end, it will earn a PRA of six BP.  
The foregoing metrics and corresponding Regulatory Liabilities and PRAs, will continue for 
subsequent calendar years unless modified by the Commission.
b.
Leak-Prone Distribution Pipe Replacement 
The Company shall replace or remove a total of 28 miles of leak prone pipe for a three-
year period (2022 through 2024 combined), with the annual replacement mileage at the 
12 
Unless otherwise indicated, all references to Basis Points in this document are pre-tax Basis Points.

Company’s discretion, provided that not less than 8.0 miles will be replaced in any given year.

prone distribution pipe in each of CY 2022, CY 2023 and CY 2024 with a total of 28.0 miles 
over the three-year period 2022 through 2024, then Corning LDC will be subject to a maximum 
annual Regulatory Liability of 15 BP. The Company shall replace 250 leak-prone services per 
year and these replacements shall occur concurrent with the main replacement efforts.  For net 
plant reconciliation purposes, compliance with these targets will be determined on a cumulative 
basis.  For years subsequent to CY 2024,13 Corning LDC shall replace or remove not less than 
7.0 miles per year until LPP replacement or removal is completed or until that target is changed 
by the Commission.  The Signatory Parties agree that Corning LDC shall have the right to file a

e.
Compliance with Pipeline Safety Regulations
i.
Staff Audit and Identification of Risk Categories
Consistent with the provisions of the 2021 Rate Order, Corning LDC will incur a 
Regulatory Liability for each occurrence, in excess of the thresholds described herein, of 
noncompliance with (i.e., violation of) certain gas safety regulations identified during Staff’s 
annual records and field audits.  Each calendar year Staff performs a record and field audit to 
determine whether Corning LDC is in compliance with the provisions of 16 NYCRR Parts 255 
and 261.  Each audit concludes with a letter from Staff, sent to Corning and filed with the 
Secretary to the Commission, listing occurrences of noncompliance with pertinent code sections.  
Areas subject to audit include those tasks having varying degrees of risk.  This measure focuses 
on those code sections considered by Staff to represent “High Risk,” as identified in 
Appendix N, as well as on those considered by Staff to have lesser levels of risk (“Other Risk”), 
as identified in Appendix N.  Staff’s categorization of violations as “High Risk” or “Other Risk” 
is for administrative purposes of this measure and does not constitute an admission by the 
Company as to the level of risk associated with any charged violation.  Each instance of a High 
Risk or Other Risk violation shall be counted as an individual occurrence; however, for purposes 
of assessing a violation of the Company’s O&M Plan, the associated violation of the Company’s 
O&M Plan shall only be deemed as one occurrence, if the same occurrence is also cited under 
the technical code section. 


--- Page 30 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
24 
ii.
Regulatory Liability per Violation Occurrence
As shown in Appendix N, in each of CY 2022 through CY 2024, Corning LDC shall 
have a maximum Regulatory Liability exposure of 75 BP per year.  The 75 BP will be assessed 
as follows.  For Record Audits, the first five High Risk violations will not subject Corning LDC 
to any adjustment; from the sixth to the 20th High Risk violations, the Company will incur one-
half BP per violation; and for each High Risk violation in excess of 20, the Company will incur 
one BP per violation.  For the first 15 Other Risk violations, Corning LDC will not be subject to 
any adjustment; and, for each Other Risk violation in excess of 15, the Company will incur a 
Regulatory Liability of one-fourth BP per violation.  For Field Audits and Investigations, the 
Company will incur a Regulatory Liability of one-half BP for each of the first 20 High Risk 
violations; and, for each High Risk violation in excess of 20, the Company will incur a 
Regulatory Liability of one BP per violation; and, for all Other Risk violations, the Company 
will incur a Regulatory Liability of one-fourth BP.    The number of occurrences of non-
compliance with each High Risk and Other Risk regulation listed in Appendix N subject to a 
negative revenue adjustment is capped at ten per audit type (Field or Records) per calendar year. 
If Corning LDC is cited for more than ten occurrences of non-compliance with a particular High 
Risk or Other Risk regulation in a calendar year, Corning LDC will file with the Secretary to the 
Commission a compliance improvement plan that contains: (1) a root cause analysis of Corning 
LDC’s compliance deficiency and (2) a proposed mitigation plan to address future performance.  
Corning LDC and Staff will meet to develop a mutually agreeable mitigation plan, which will 
include provisions for tracking and regular reporting on Corning LDC’s efforts to address the 
compliance deficiency.  The compliance improvement plan will be filed with the Secretary to the 
Commission within 90 days of Staff’s audit letter identifying the compliance deficiency.  Should 


--- Page 31 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
25 
the Company fail to comply with its compliance improvement plan, those violations of a code 
section in excess of ten that were previously capped shall count towards this metric. 
iii.
Effective Date and Applicable Audit
The foregoing metrics for High Risk and Other Risk Level Safety Requirements will be 
effective as of January 1, 2022, and will be measured on a CY basis.17  These metrics will remain 
in effect until changed by the Commission. 
iv.
Procedural Details
At the conclusion of each audit, Staff and Corning LDC will have a compliance meeting 
where Staff will present its findings to the Company.  The Company will have ten business days 
from the date the audit findings are presented to cure any identified document deficiency.  Only 
official Company records, as defined in the Company’s O&M Plan, will be considered by Staff 
as a cure to a document deficiency.  Staff will submit its final audit report to the Secretary to the 
Commission under Case 21-G-0394.  If Corning LDC disputes any of Staff’s final audit results, 
the Company may appeal Staff’s finding to the Commission.  The Company reserves the right to 
appeal on whatever grounds it reasonably believes the Commission may deem appropriate and 
relevant.  The Company will not incur a Regulatory Liability on the contested findings until such 
time as the Commission has issued a final decision on the contested findings.  The Company 
does not waive its right to seek an appeal of any Commission determination regarding a violation 
or penalty under applicable law. 
If Corning LDC receives a fine from the Commission under PSL Section 25 for a 
violation of 16 NYCRR Parts 255 or 261 in a separate proceeding, the occurrence(s) for which 
17 
For example, violations found for the audit of field activities conducted by Staff in 2022 and for the audit of 
the 2022 records conducted by Staff in 2023 will constitute the CY 2022 audit.

7.
Customer Service
a.
Customer Service Performance Indicators
The Signatory Parties have agreed to certain modifications to the Customer Service 
Performance Indicators (“CSPI”) that were most recently adopted in the 2021 Rate Order.  The 
three currently operative CSPI metrics are for Customer Complaints, Customer Satisfaction 
Survey (“CSS”) and Keeping Scheduled Appointments. 
i.
Customer Complaints and Customer Satisfaction
Survey
For Customer Complaints, Corning LDC must have three or fewer escalated complaints18
per year to avoid a Regulatory Liability of 15 pre-tax BP.  For the Customer Satisfaction Survey, 
the Company must maintain a score of 87.7% or greater to avoid a Regulatory Liability.  If that 
score falls between 86.7% and less than or equal to 87.7%, a Regulatory Liability of five BP will 
be imposed; if the score is between 85.7% and less than or equal to 86.7%, a Regulatory Liability 
of 10 BP will be imposed; and, if the score is less than 85.7%, the Regulatory Liability will be 15 
BP.  For the foregoing two metrics, Customer Complaints and CSS, if the Merger Transaction is 
approved, the Regulatory Liabilities will be increased by doubling the BP amounts indicated.  
Corning LDC’s annual CSPI report, filed 60 days after the end of each Rate Year, will include 
the Company’s performance on both of these metrics.   
18  
For clarity, “escalated complaints,” as used herein, are Annual PSC Escalated Complaints, defined as those 
complaints that are escalated for further handling and investigation because the customer informed Staff’s 
Office of Consumer Services (“OCS”) that Corning LDC failed to satisfy his/her initial complaint after OCS 
forwarded the initial complaint to the Company, and the results of an annual CSS. 


--- Page 33 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
27 
ii.
Keeping Scheduled Appointments 
The currently applicable metric for addressing missed appointments will be continued.  
To the extent the Company fails to keep a scheduled appointment with a customer, it shall 
provide a $25 bill credit to the customer for each such instance; but such credit will not be 
available to the customer if the customer cancels the appointment, is not available for the 
appointment, or otherwise is responsible for the missed appointment.  The Company will provide 
in its annual CSPI report a summary of the number of missed appointments and the total cost of 
bill credit disbursements.  
b.
Residential Service Terminations, Arrearages and 
Uncollectibles 
Corning LDC’s performance mechanism based on metrics for residential service 
terminations, arrearages and uncollectibles was suspended by the 2021 Rate Order.  The 
Signatory Parties agree that the performance mechanism shall remain suspended until addressed 
by the Commission in the Company’s next base rate case.
c.
Low Income Program 
As indicated in Section IV.B.3.f, above, the Signatory Parties agree that Corning LDC’s 
annual budget for its Low Income Program, which provides monthly credits for qualifying 
residential low-income customers, for each of the three Rate Years of the Rate Plan will be as

--- Page 37 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
31 
b.
True-Up Procedure
A downward only net plant true-up shall be used to determine if Corning LDC has spent 
its capital budget in each Rate Year.  The Company shall file with the Secretary of the 
Commission, 120 days following the end of each Rate Year, a net plant analysis to determine if 
Corning LDC shall defer a customer benefit.  The annual net plant target is developed by taking 
the forecast Rate Year average of the monthly averages of:  (1) plant in service, (2) CWIP, 
(3) deferred taxes associated with tax depreciation, accelerated recovery of plant and
contributions in aid of construction (“CIAC”), and (4) depreciation reserve including accelerated 
recovery of plant.  The development of and resulting net plant targets for each Rate Year are 
shown in Appendix P. 
The net plant targets for each Rate Year shall be compared to the actual net plant during 
such Rate Year.  The actual Rate Year net plant shall be determined by taking the actual average 
of the monthly averages of: (1) plant in service; (2) CWIP; (3) deferred taxes associated with tax 
depreciation, accelerated recovery of plant and CIAC; and (4) depreciation reserve including 
accelerated recovery of plant.  If the actual net plant in service falls short of the target net plant in 
service for a particular Rate Year, Corning LDC shall defer carrying costs for customers’ benefit 
equal to the shortfall multiplied by the authorized pre-tax rate of return, as well as depreciation 
expense associated with the shortfall.  The deferral shall accrue interest at the Company’s 
authorized pre-tax rate of return.  The depreciation expense associated with the shortfall will be 
calculated by dividing the actual depreciation expense for the Rate Year by the average plant 
balance to develop a composite depreciation rate, which will then be multiplied by the shortfall.  
If the actual net plant in service exceeds the target net plant in service for a particular Rate Year, 
no adjustment (i.e., no surcharge to customers) shall be made. 


--- Page 38 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
32 
There will be a cumulative test at the end of Rate Year 3 to determine the final carrying 
charges owed to customers.  In conjunction with the Company’s Rate Year 3 net plant analysis, 
as explained above, the Company will conduct a cumulative carrying cost analysis as shown in 
Appendix P.  For each Rate Year, the Company will calculate the carrying costs and 
accumulated interest.  If the sum of the carrying costs and accumulated interest over the three 
Rate Years results in a shortfall, the amount is owed to customers.  If there is a net excess over 
the three Rate Years, no adjustment (i.e., no surcharge to customers) shall be made. 
9.
Lost and Unaccounted For Gas
Beginning September 1, 2022, the system-wide fixed factor of adjustment (“FOA”) shall 
remain at 1.0027.  The actual lost and unaccounted for (“LAUF”) calculation will be total 
metered into the system divided by total metered out of the system with no adjustments.  The 
dead band limits shall remain at the lower dead band limit of 1.0000 and at the upper dead band 
limit of 1.0192.  All firm customers will be charged or credited, as the case may be, for 
differences between the fixed FOA and the actual FOA within the dead band.  The Company will 
not recover the portion of the actual losses beyond the upper limit of the dead band.  In the event 
the Company’s actual FOA goes below 1.0000, all resulting benefits beyond the dead band will 
be awarded to all firm customers.  The dead band limits will become effective beginning 
September 1, 2022.  The FOA and the dead band limits are fixed throughout the Rate Plan or 
until changed by the Commission.  The LAUF incentive surcharge or refund will be collected 
through the DRA.  The calculation of the FOA and dead band limits is shown in detail in 
Appendix Q. 


--- Page 39 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
33 
10.
Revenue Decoupling Mechanism
The RDM adopted in the 2021 Rate Order will continue to apply to Residential 
Customers taking service under current Corning LDC Service Classification (“SC”) 1 and 
Corning LDC SC 14.  The RDM targets are shown in Appendix R.  
11.
Off-System Sales and Capacity Release Revenues
The Company shall be permitted to retain 15% of any off-system and/or capacity release 
revenues generated via assets it does not release to a third party and the third party is not 
compensated or allowed to profit at the expense of the firm ratepayer from the transaction using 
such assets.  In the event that the Company engages a compensated third party (e.g., an asset 
manager) and the cost of such engagement is recovered in rates, the Company will not share in 
the revenues generated by such third party through off-system sales and capacity release 
transactions.

13.
Gas Programs and Supply
a.
Preparation of Strategic Plan for Decarbonization
Within 120 days of the issuance of the Commission’s Order approving this Joint 
Proposal, Corning LDC will file with the Secretary to the Commission a draft Strategic Plan for 
Decarbonization designed to promote and/or implement, consistent with the Company’s limited

b.
Greenhouse Gas Emission Data
Not later than the date on which Corning LDC files its Statement in Support of this Joint 
Proposal, the Company will file with the Secretary to the Commission its estimates of the 
greenhouse gas (“GHG”) emissions attributable to the Company’s natural gas operations in the 
State of New York to be used as a baseline for evolving efforts to reduce GHG emissions.  The 
Signatory Parties acknowledge that Corning LDC has limited resources to devote to this new 
requirement and will not be expected to retain consultants, reassign employees or purchase 
information or software for this purpose.  The Signatory Parties agree that, for reporting carbon 
dioxide emissions, the Company may rely upon its regular e-GGRT Reporting Year Comparison 
Report to the Environmental Protection Agency (“EPA”), and, for reporting methane emissions, 
the Company may base its estimates on the current LAUF factor. 
V.
TERMS GOVERNING THE MERGER CASE
A.
Accounting and Finance
1.
Management and Operation
a.
Independent Corning Holdco Board Member
Corning Holdco and Corning LDC will continue to have a board of governance structure 


--- Page 41 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
35 
that in all material respects will be the same as the structures that exists today.  Corning Holdco 
will ensure that its Board of Directors has at least one independent, local board member who 
resides within Corning LDC’s service area or has substantial and on-going ties to the service 
area, which include one or more of the following:  employment within the service area, 
ownership interest, along with a substantial physical presence, in a business operating or 
headquartered within the Company’s service territory that affords the individual knowledge and 
understanding of the issues and concerns of people in the service territory, and employment by or 
substantial involvement in the management of a not-for-profit, educational, charitable or civic 
entity operating or headquartered within the service area. 
Corning Holdco will provide notice and certification to the Secretary of the Commission 
regarding the appointment(s) made to Corning Holdco and Corning LDC board of directors.  
b.
Location of Headquarters, Books and Records
The headquarters, together with the books and records, of Corning LDC will remain 
within the Company’s service area for a minimum of five years.  After that period has expired, 
Corning LDC must obtain approval from the Commission to relocate either Corning LDC’s 
headquarters or books and records outside the service area. 
c.
Collective Bargaining Agreement and Employment Levels
Upon closing of the Merger Transaction and for at least 12 months thereafter, ACP 
Crotona: (1) shall not permit a net reduction, due to involuntary attrition as a result of the Merger 
Transaction, in the employment levels at Corning LDC, and (2) ACP Crotona will cause Corning 
LDC to provide employees of Corning LDC compensation and benefits (including retirement 
benefits; provided vested rights under the defined benefit pension plan will continue to be 
maintained in accordance with applicable legal requirements) that are at least as favorable in the 


--- Page 42 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
36 
aggregate as the compensation and benefits provided to those employees immediately before 
closing of the Merger Transaction. 
If, during that 12-month period, a current employee voluntarily leaves his or her position, 
and that specific position is needed to support the provision of safe and reliable service, Corning 
LDC will fill the position with an individual qualified for the position.  
ACP Crotona will honor the existing collective bargaining agreement with Corning 
LDC’s union employees. 
2.
Goodwill and Acquisition Costs
a.
Prohibition on Pass-Through in Rates
Corning LDC will not be permitted to recover goodwill (i.e., premium above book value) 
or the costs of the Merger Transaction in rates, including, but not limited to, costs related to 
brokers’ fees, insurance, consulting or advisory costs, accounting fees, financing, or legal fees. 
b.
Prohibition on Reflecting Goodwill and Acquisition Costs
Corning LDC and ACP Crotona will not be permitted to reflect or pass along goodwill or 
the costs of the Merger Transaction on its books and records, and such costs will be excluded 
from rate base, expense and capitalization in the determination of Corning LDC’s revenue 
requirements or any other component for rates and earned return for New York State regulatory 
reporting purposes. 
c.
Goodwill Impairment Analysis
If, at any time after the closing of the Merger Transaction, an analysis determines that the 
goodwill from the Merger Transaction residing on the Corning Holdco books and records, or the 
goodwill already reflected on the books and records from prior transactions, is impaired to any 
extent, Corning LDC, within 45 days from the date of such determination, shall submit such 


--- Page 43 ---
37 
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
analysis to the Secretary to the Commission.  For purposes of this provision, a determination of 
impairment means a final determination after Corning Holdco’s independent auditors have 
reviewed Corning Holdco's determination that impairment exists and have agreed to the amount 
of the impairment that has been recorded on Corning Holdco’s books. 
d. 
Post-Closing Report
Within 90 days after closing of the Merger Transaction, Corning LDC will submit to the 
Secretary to the Commission a post-closing report detailing the costs associated with the Corning 
LDC employees working on either the Merger Case or involved in transition planning and 
specifying the accounts in which those costs were recorded.  Such costs will be excluded from 
any earnings calculation that is required by the ESM applicable to Corning LDC. 
e. 
Cost Tracking
Corning LDC will track the costs and benefits resulting directly from the Merger 
Transaction, including but not limited to operational changes. 
f. 
Post-Closing Journal Entries
Within 90 days of the closing of the Merger Transaction, Corning LDC will file with the 
Secretary to the Commission the post-closing journal entries pertaining to the Merger 
Transaction, together with all supporting documentation including but not limited to the amount 
of goodwill and all transaction costs. 
3.

Corning LDC will continue to adhere to the ring-fencing measures that were adopted by

the Commission as part of its approval of a Joint Proposal in Case 12-G-0141,20 except to the

b.
Minimum Common Equity Ratio 
Consistent with the May 2013 Order in Case 12-G-0141, the following restrictions on the 
payment of dividends shall remain applicable.  At each month-end, Corning LDC will maintain a 
minimum common equity ratio (measured using a trailing 13-month average) of no less than 300 
BP below the common equity ratio used to set rates.  If the Company’s actual common equity 
ratio falls below this level, the Company will be prohibited from paying dividends to Corning 
Holdco until the Company again meets or exceeds this threshold.  If Corning LDC’s actual 
common equity ratio (measured using a trailing 13-month average) is between 200 and 300 BP 
below the common equity ratio used to set rates, the payment of dividends to Corning Holdco 
will be restricted to 50% of regulated net income, as calculated on a two-year rolling average 
basis.  If Corning LDC’s actual common equity ratio (measured using a trailing 13-month 
average) is less than 200 BP below the common equity ratio used to set rates, the payment of 
dividends to Corning Holdco will be restricted to 90% of regulated net income, as calculated on 
a two-year rolling average basis.  If the Commission authorizes a common equity ratio that is 
below Corning LDC’s actual common equity ratio at the time rates are reset, the Company will

c.
Money Pooling 
Consistent with the May 2013 Order in Case 12-G-0141, the following restrictions on 
money pooling will remain in place.  More specifically, Corning LDC may participate in a 
money pool only if all other participants, with the exception of Corning Holdco, are regulated 
utilities operating within the United States, in which case Corning LDC may participate as either 
a borrower or a lender.  Corning Holdco may participate in a money pool involving Corning 
LDC, but only as a lender.  Corning LDC is prohibited from participating, directly, or indirectly, 
in any money pool in which any participant loans or transfers funds to Corning Holdco.

Consistent with the May 2013 Order in Case 12-G-0141, Corning LDC will maintain 
banking, committed credit facilities, and cash management arrangements that are separate from 
the Company’s affiliates.  Corning LDC will not lend to, guarantee, or financially support 
Corning Holdco, ACP Crotona, Argo Infrastructure Partners LP (“Argo”),21 or any of their 
affiliates.  Furthermore, Corning LDC will not engage in, provide financial support to, guarantee, 
or be responsible financially for any non-regulated businesses, except as authorized by the 
Commission.  Neither Corning Holdco, ACP Crotona, Argo, nor any of their affiliates may enter 
into any cross-default provisions that affect Corning LDC in any manner. 
e.
Credit Metrics 
Corning LDC shall be required to maintain its ratio of Funds from Operations (“FFO”) to

ACP Crotona and ACP Merger Sub are affiliates of Argo and are part of a long-duration investment

Debt at or above 9.0% and its ratio of Debt to Earnings Before Interest, Taxes, Depreciation, and

For the purposes of calculating these ratios, “Debt” shall be defined only as referring to Corning

In the event that Corning LDC falls short of maintaining these ratios within five years 
following the close of the Merger Transaction, then the cost of any debt subsequently issued 
during such five-year period will be capped at an imputed cost rate for ratemaking purposes.  
The imputed cost rate shall be based on the yield of “BBB”/“Baa” rated utility debt.23  The 
difference between the actual cost of the debt issued and the imputed cost rate shall be 
disallowed in rates and deemed unrecoverable from customers.24  The imputed cost rate shall be 
calculated as the average yield on “Baa” rated public utility bonds reported by Mergent Bond 
Record,25 less the spread between the yield on the 30-year Treasury bond and the yield on the 
Treasury bond most closely approximating the term of the debt issued by Corning LDC.  An 
example of how this imputed cost rate shall be calculated is included in Appendix V.

Holdco and its other utility affiliates, to the extent those books and records relate to the 
operations of Corning LDC.  Such access will be sufficient to provide Staff with the opportunity 
to assess that costs are allocated properly among the affiliates of Corning Holdco, ACP Crotona 
and ACP Crotona Holdings, rather than being allocated downstream to Corning LDC.  As used 
herein, “sufficiency” refers only to documentation that is adequate to support the allocation of

other needed information. 
ACP Crotona and ACP Crotona Holdings will, upon request, provide Staff with access to 
their accounting policies that pertain to Corning LDC. 
Appendix W to this Joint Proposal provides a listing of the internal controls and related 
accounting procedures that Corning LDC has in place pre-merger for voluntary compliance with 
Sarbanes-Oxley requirements and is committed to continue to follow after the Merger 
Transaction is consummated.

In the event that Argo should complete any additional mergers or acquisitions within the 
State of New York, subject to Commission jurisdiction, before the Commission issues an order 
approving new rates for Corning LDC, any savings attributable to Corning LDC or its customers 
will be deferred for the benefit of the Company’s customers. 
8.
Merger Savings and Public Benefit Adjustment 
a.
Merger Savings 
The avoided cost savings resulting from Corning Holdco’s no longer having to incur 
certain costs following the consummation of the Merger Transaction (the “Merger Savings”) 
total approximately $300,000 per year (including inflation).  These Merger Savings will be used 
to offset the revenue requirement established in the Rate Case and are reflected in Appendix A. 
b.
Public Benefit Adjustment 
A PBA, separate from the Merger Savings referenced above, will be provided to 
customers in the total amount of $1,200,000 during the term of the Rate Plan.  $400,000 per year 
will be used to offset the revenue requirement established in the Rate Case and is reflected in 


--- Page 51 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
45 
Appendix A.  Of the total amount,  $210,00028 will be directly assigned  to fund the additional 
expenditures resulting from the proposed increases in credits for the Low-Income Program, as 
described in Section IV.C.7.c, above. 
B.
Market Power 
ACP Crotona and ACP Holding shall notify the Secretary to the Commission of any 
future acquisition by Argo or its affiliates that can affect natural gas supply or pricing in Corning 
LDC’s service area. 
C.
Reliability 
To keep the Commission and Staff aware of changes in staffing or procedures that would 
be expected to have an impact on supply and/or reliability, Corning LDC will notify Staff in the 
Reliability section of the Office of Electric, Gas and Water as early as feasible of any such 
changes and their expected impact.  The emphasis of this requirement is on changes that would 
not likely require notification in the absence of this provision, and on providing advance notice 
whenever possible.29
Corning LDC will continue to focus on maximizing the Company’s gas supply and 
transportation assets, including by consideration of opportunities to employ Asset Management 
Agreements.  This provision is not to be construed, however, as a requirement that the Company 
use Asset Management Agreements. 
In order to ensure continued system reliability, the Company has committed to capital 
budgets for the duration of the Rate Plan.  The amount of the capital budget through FY 2025 is 
28  
$70,000 per year over three years. 
29  
Examples of developments contemplated by this provision are changes in responsibilities of individuals 
involved in supply and/or reliability functions; hirings, terminations and retirements within those functional 
areas; and retention or termination of contractors or consultants involved in such functions. 


--- Page 52 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
46 
detailed in Appendix O.  For the years 2026 and 2027, the Company commits to a capital budget 
of at least $5.8 million. 
The Signatory Parties recognize that there is a need for the Commission to comply with 
applicable provisions of the Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (“CLCPA”),30
while, at the same time, ensuring compliance with the provisions of the PSL and other applicable 
requirements regarding the availability, cost, safety and reliability of gas service provided by 
local distribution companies (“LDCs”) such as Corning LDC.  The Gas Programs outlined in 
Section IV.B.13, above, and in Appendix U are intended to be consistent with the objectives of 
the CLCPA and to maintain a balance between those objectives and the pre-existing and 
continuing obligations of LDCs such as the Company.  The Joint Petitioners acknowledge that 
the objectives and obligations under the CLCPA are evolving and will require continued 
attention. 
D.
Consumer Policy 
Within 90 days following the end of each calendar year ending during the term of the 
Rate Plan, Corning LDC will file with the Secretary to the Commission a summary report that 
will describe the Customer Service operations the Company has undertaken during that year and 
identifying any changes the Company proposes to make to its Customer Service operations.  
Following review of the report, Staff will provide its feedback to the Company at a mutually 
agreed time and in a mutually agreed form. 
30  
L. 2019, Ch. 106. 


--- Page 53 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
47 
E.
Community Engagement 
Within 90 days following the end of each calendar year ending during the term of the 
Rate Plan, Corning LDC will file with the Secretary to the Commission a report that will 
describe the community involvement activities the Company has undertaken during that year and 
the associated spending on each activity.  Such report will identify newly implemented activities, 
as well as improvements to pre-existing activities.  The report may, but is not required to, include 
activities that are not funded by Corning LDC’s customers. 
F.
Outreach and Education 
Within 30 days of the closing of the Merger Transaction, Corning LDC and ACP Crotona 
will submit for Staff review a draft plan regarding Outreach and Education (“O&E”) specific to 
the Merger Transaction.  Upon receipt, Staff will review the draft plan and any proposed 
revisions resulting from such review will be discussed with Corning LDC and included in 
Corning LDC’s 2022 O&E Plan.  The draft plan will include discussion of any changes brought 
about by the Merger Transaction that will affect customers, with the final plan detailing any 
material provisions set forth in the Commission’s order approving the Merger Transaction. 
VI.
DISPOSITION OF ARTICLE 78 PROCEEDING 
The Signatory Parties agree that, upon the issuance of an order approving this Joint 
Proposal without modification of any substantive provision hereof, Corning LDC will enter into 
good-faith negotiations with the Commission to resolve the pending case, In the Matter of 
Corning Natural Gas Corporation v. Public Service Commission of the State of New York
(Supreme Court, Albany County, Index No. 906009-21), currently pending before the New York 
Appellate Division, Third Department (the “Article 78 Proceeding”), which was commenced by 
the Company in response to the Commission’s 2021 Rate Order.  In the event that settlement 


--- Page 54 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
48 
negotiations result in an outcome entitling Corning LDC to a monetary recovery, Corning LDC 
will defer any such recovery until new rates are set in the Company’s next base rate case.  In 
addition, Corning LDC will forgo recovery of any carrying charges on the amount subject to 
such recovery that otherwise might accrue during the period prior to the effective date of rates 
approved in the next base rate case.  Irrespective of whether settlement negotiations are initiated 
pursuant to this Section or otherwise, in the event that the Article 78 Proceeding is litigated, 
Corning LDC agrees that, notwithstanding any right the Company may have upon judicial 
resolution in its favor, the Company will defer any recovery of funds until rates are set in the 
Company’s next base rate case and Corning LDC will not seek to recover carrying changes on 
the amount subject to recovery that otherwise might accrue for the period prior to the date such 
new base rates become effective.  To permit settlement negotiations to have the maximum 
opportunity to succeed, the Company and the Commission will cooperate in seeking extensions 
of the Appellate Division, Third Department’s deadline(s) for perfecting the Article 78 
Proceeding, including the filing of briefs. 
VII.
GENERAL PROVISIONS 
A.
Rate Changes; Reservation of Authority 
Except pursuant to rate changes permitted by this Section, Corning LDC will not file 
rates to become effective prior to July 1, 2025.  Nothing herein precludes Corning LDC from 
filing a new base rate case for rates to become effective on or after July 1, 2025. 
Changes to Corning LDC's base delivery service rates during the term of the Rate Plan 
will not be permitted, except for the changes provided for or detailed in this Joint Proposal, and, 
subject to Commission approval, changes as a result of the following circumstances:  (1) upon 
the occurrence, at any time, of circumstances that in the judgment of the Commission so 


--- Page 55 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
49 
threaten, Corning LDC’s economic viability or ability to maintain safe, reliable and adequate 
service as to warrant an exception to the limitations on rate changes provided for or detailed in 
this Joint Proposal, Corning LDC will be permitted to file for an increase in base delivery service 
rates; and (2) the Signatory Parties recognize that the Company reserves the ability to petition the 
Commission and the Commission reserves the authority to act on the level of Corning LDC’s 
rates in the event of unforeseen circumstances that, in the Commission's opinion, have such a 
substantial impact on the range of earnings levels or equity costs envisioned by this Joint 
Proposal as to render Corning LDC’s rates unjust or unreasonable or insufficient for the 
provision of safe and adequate service.  The Signatory Parties reserve the right to oppose any 
filings made by the Company under this Section. 
B.
Impact of Mandatory Changes 
To the extent that mandatory changes not specifically addressed herein due to generic 
policy decisions of the Commission become effective during the Rate Period covered by the 
Joint Proposal, the revenue requirement impacts of revenues, expenses and rate base (including 
income or other federal or State tax expense) for each specific change will be quantified 
separately and, to the extent that each separate item increases or decreases the utility cost of 
service by an amount greater than 5% of gas pre-tax utility income annually, it shall be deferred, 
with interest accruing at the unadjusted customer deposit rate, for refund to or recovery from 
customers in a manner to be determined by the Commission.  The same treatment shall be 
afforded any externally imposed accounting change; any change in federal, state or local rates, 
regulation, or precedent governing income, revenue, sales, fees, or franchise taxes; or any 
legislative, court, or regulatory change, which imposes or modifies existing obligations or duties, 
including but not limited to implementation of climate-related restrictions and costs.  In the event 


--- Page 56 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
50 
that these changes occur, Corning LDC shall file a letter with the Secretary to the Commission 
setting forth the rationale for the deferral and its calculation.  Consistent with the foregoing, the 
Signatory Parties recognize that generic policy decisions of the Commission will be applicable to 
Corning LDC according to their terms unless stayed by the Commission or a court or provided 
otherwise by the Commission during the Rate Period.  All Signatory Parties reserve all of their 
administrative and judicial rights in connection with such generic proceedings. 
C.
Provisions Not Separable 
The Signatory Parties intend this Joint Proposal to be a complete resolution of all the 
issues in Cases 21-G-0394 and 21-G-0260, and the Company will file tariffs in a manner 
consistent with the terms of this Joint Proposal.  The terms of this Joint Proposal are submitted as 
an integrated whole.  In the event or to the extent that the Commission does not adopt the terms 
of this Joint Proposal in their entirety, the Signatory Parties shall be free to pursue their 
respective positions in this proceeding without prejudice, and this Joint Proposal shall not be 
used in evidence or cited against any such Signatory Party or used for any other purpose.  It is 
also understood that each provision of this Joint Proposal is in consideration and support of all 
the other provisions, and expressly conditioned upon acceptance by the Commission.  Except as 
set forth herein, none of the Signatory Parties is deemed to have approved, agreed to or 
consented to any principle, methodology or interpretation of law underlying or supposed to 
underlie any provision herein.   
D.
Provisions Not Precedent 
The terms and provisions of this Joint Proposal apply solely to, and are binding only in 
the context of the purposes and results of this Joint Proposal.  None of the terms or provisions of 
this Joint Proposal, including any methodology or principle utilized herein, and none of the 


--- Page 57 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
51 
positions taken herein by any Signatory Party may be referred to, cited, or relied upon by any 
other party in any fashion as precedent or otherwise in any other proceeding before this 
Commission or any other regulatory agency or before any court of law for any purpose other 
than furtherance of the purposes, results, and disposition of matters governed by this Joint 
Proposal.  Concessions made by Signatory Parties on various issues included in this Joint 
Proposal do not preclude those parties from addressing such issues in other proceedings.  This 
Joint Proposal shall not be construed, interpreted or otherwise deemed in any respect to 
constitute an admission by any Signatory Party regarding any allegations, contentions or issues 
raised in this proceeding or addressed in this Joint Proposal. 
E.
Submission of Proposal 
Each Signatory Party agrees to submit this Joint Proposal to the Commission, to support 
and request its adoption by the Commission, and not to take a position in this proceeding 
contrary to the agreements set forth herein or to assist another participant in taking such a 
contrary position in these proceedings.  The Signatory Parties believe that the resolution of the 
issues, as set forth in this Joint Proposal, is just and reasonable and otherwise in accordance with 
the PSL, the Commission's regulations and applicable Commission precedent.  The Signatory 
Parties believe that this Joint Proposal will satisfy the requirements of PSL § 65(1), that Corning 
provide safe and adequate service at just and reasonable rates, as well as the requirements of PSL 
§ 70 that the Merger Transaction be in the public interest.  Any Signatory Party subscribing to 
some, but not all, of the provisions of this Joint Proposal agrees not to oppose the provisions to 
which it has not subscribed. 


--- Page 58 ---
CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal 
52 
F.
Effect of Commission Adoption of Terms of this Proposal 
No provision of this Joint Proposal or the Commission's adoption of the terms of this 
Joint Proposal shall in any way abrogate or limit the Commission's statutory authority under the 
PSL.  The Signatory Parties recognize that any Commission adoption of the terms of this Joint 
Proposal does not waive the Commission's ongoing rights and responsibilities to enforce its 
orders and effectuate the goals expressed therein, nor the rights and responsibilities of Staff to 
conduct investigations or take other actions in furtherance of its duties and responsibilities. 
G.
Further Assurances 
The Signatory Parties recognize that certain provisions of this Joint Proposal require that 
actions be taken in the future to fully effectuate this Joint Proposal.  Accordingly, the Signatory 
Parties agree to cooperate with each other in good faith in taking such actions.  In the event of 
any disagreement over the interpretation of this Joint Proposal or implementation of any of the 
provisions of this Joint Proposal, which cannot be resolved informally among the Signatory 
Parties, such disagreement shall be resolved in the following manner:  (a) the Signatory Parties 
shall promptly convene a conference and in good faith attempt to resolve any such disagreement; 
and (b) if any such disagreement cannot be resolved by the Signatory Parties, any Signatory 
Party may petition the Commission for resolution of the disputed matter. 
H.
Execution 
This Joint Proposal may be executed in one or more counterparts, all of which taken 
together shall constitute one and the same instrument which shall be binding upon each 
Signatory Party when it is executed in counterpart, filed with the Secretary of the Commission 
and approved by the Commission.

CASES 21-G-0394 & 21-G-0260 – Joint Proposal

I.
Entire Agreement 
This Joint Proposal, including all attachments, exhibits and appendices, if any, represents 
the entire agreement of the Signatory Parties with respect to the matters resolved herein.

--- Page 201 ---
1 
 
Cases 21-G-0260 and 21-G-0394 
 
 
 
 
 
Appendix U 
 
CLCPA Related Issues 
 
 
1. Specific actions to be taken by the Company, consistent with its limited personnel 
resources compared to other utilities, include preparing a Strategic Plan for 
Decarbonization.  This plan shall contain the following components: 
a) Company stopping active, unsolicited gas marketing activities. 
b) Exploring the potential use of low carbon alternatives.  The Company has 
previously identified possible sources of Renewable Natural Gas (RNG).  The 
plan shall include details regarding bringing RNG into the Company’s system.  
This will include what those sources are, where they are located, how will 
they be integrated into the distribution system and when these sources will 
come onto the system. 
c) Developing demand response programs.  Providing inquiring customers with 
contact information for the energy efficiency programs of the customer’s  
electric utility, NYSERDA and Municipal Electric Utilities Association of 
New York State (MEUA), including Bath. 
d) Providing inquiring customers with contact information for heating 
electrification programs offered by local electric utility. 
 
The Company shall file its draft plan as specified in the body of this Joint 
Proposal. 
2. NPAs 
a) Line Extensions - Corning shall consider alternatives to any proposed line 
extension exceeding 500 feet, including referring customers to clean heat 
programs conducted by applicable electric utilities.  Corning will consult with 
DPS Staff prior to construction of line extensions exceeding 500 feet and will 
file a report with the Secretary to the Commission after consulting with Staff 
to indicate the cost of any proposed extension more than 500 feet in length 
and what alternatives were considered.  The report will also include how 
many potential customers would be served by the line extension and 
documentation that Corning informed the potential customers of alternatives 
to natural gas heating. 
 
b) Demand Side Management (DSM) – In cooperation with NYSERDA and 
MEUA, the Company shall explore the availability and potential benefits of 
DSM, with emphasis on low-income customers.  The Company shall report its 
findings as specified in the body of this Joint Proposal 


--- Page 202 ---
2 
 
 
3. Energy Efficiency 
a) The Company shall encourage gas conservation via messaging materials to its 
existing customers.  
b) The Company will maintain and update the portion of its website that contain 
links to refer customers to other entities and resources that can assist 
customers with reducing energy consumption and saving money on their bills. 
The Company shall actively direct customers to these resources through its 
regular consumer outreach and education materials and any other relevant 
customer contact points. 
c) The Company will refer its new gas applicants to the NYS Clean Heat 
Program Manager for New York State Electric and Gas Corporation:  
 
Nicole Williams – Program Manager 
 
 
Phone: (585) 484-6592 
 
 
Email: Nicole.williams@nyseg.com 
 
 
https://saveenergy.ny.gov/NYScleanheat/nys-electric-utility-contact-information/ 
 
d) Within 60 days after the end of each rate year, the Company shall provide the 
Secretary to the Commission a report identifying the number of new 
applicants for service it received and the energy efficiency program(s) for 
which information was made available to those applicants.  Of the applicants 
who were referred to other alternatives but ended up becoming a gas 
customer, identify the reason(s) why the customer ultimately decided to 
choose gas service instead of the other alternatives.  
 
 
 


--- Page 203 ---
